       if doutput is None:\\\\n                if output[sess][0].ndim == 1:\\\\n                    doutput = len(output[sess][0])\\\\n                else:\\\\n                    doutput = output[sess][0].shape[0]\\\\n            break\\\\n\\\\n    return SessionData(neural, input, output, dinput, doutput), dinput, doutput\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n# Threads converting sessions ahead of the training loop. Each holds one converted\\\\n# session, so this also caps the extra memory at that many sessions.\\\\n#\\\\n# Every task asks for at least 16 CPUs. Throughput peaks where the thread count\\\\n# matches the CPUs the container is allowed and falls away on both sides: measured\\\\n# against one thread, 8 threads gave 5.7x, 16 gave 9.0x, 24 gave 7.0x and 32 gave\\\\n# 5.8x. Past the limit the threads exhaust the cgroup\\\\'s CPU budget early in each\\\\n# period and are stopped until the next one, resuming with cold caches, which this\\\\n# strided copy is especially sensitive to.\\\\nCONVERSION_WORKERS = 16\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nclass SessionData(torch.utils.data.Dataset):\\\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Per-session tensors, converted on demand instead of all at once.\\\\n\\\\n    Indexed like the list this used to return: ``session_data[session][\\\\'neural\\\\']``.\\\\n    Converts on every call, so bind it once per session:\\\\n    ``batch = session_data[session]``.\\\\n\\\\n    Why: the previous version built every session\\\\'s float32 tensors up front while\\\\n    the caller\\\\'s numpy arrays stayed alive -- two full copies of the dataset. The\\\\n    source copy cannot be freed, because test_decoder_accuracy receives it from\\\\n    pytest\\\\'s module-scoped `submitted_data_full` fixture, which pins it for the whole\\\\n    test module. So this second copy is the only one that can be avoided. On\\\\n    mouseland/claude-code trial3 it took a 422 GB run to 984 GB and got it\\\\n    OOM-killed on a 960 GB node.\\\\n\\\\n    Args:\\\\n        neural: length nsessions; neural[session][trial] is (nneurons, T), any float\\\\n            dtype.\\\\n        input: length nsessions; input[session][trial] is (dinput,) for a per-trial\\\\n            value or (dinput, T) for a time-varying one.\\\\n        output: same convention as input, integer class labels.\\\\n        dinput: input dimension, already inferred by the caller.\\\\n        doutput: output dimension, already inferred by the caller.\\\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    def __init__(self, neural: list, input: list, output: list,\\\\n                 dinput: int, doutput: int):\\\\n        self._neural, self._input, self._output = neural, input, output\\\\n        self.dinput, self.doutput = dinput, doutput\\\\n\\\\n    def __len__(self) -> int:\\\\n        return len(self._neural)\\\\n\\\\n    def n_nonempty(self) -> int:\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Sessions holding at least one trial, counted without converting any.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n        return sum(1 for session in self._neural if len(session) > 0)\\\\n\\\\n    def iter_sessions(self, workers: int | None = None):\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Yield (session, batch) for every session, converting several at a time.\\\\n\\\\n        Conversion transposes each trial into the destination, a strided copy that\\\\n        leaves one core waiting on memory rather than saturating it, so several\\\\n        sessions convert in less time than the sum of their parts. numpy releases\\\\n        the GIL for the assignment, which is why threads suffice and no data has to\\\\n        cross a process boundary.\\\\n\\\\n        At most `workers` conversions are in flight, so the extra memory is bounded\\\\n        by that many sessions however long the dataset is.\\\\n\\\\n        Args:\\\\n            workers: threads to convert on. None uses CONVERSION_WORKERS.\\\\n\\\\n        Yields:\\\\n            (session, batch) in session order, batch as `__getitem__` returns it --\\\\n            None for a session with no trials. Order and contents match a plain\\\\n            loop over `self[session]`, so results do not depend on the thread count.\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n        if workers is None:\\\\n            workers = CONVERSION_WORKERS\\\\n        nsessions = len(self)\\\\n        if workers <= 1:\\\\n            for session in range(nsessions):\\\\n                yield session, self[session]\\\\n            return\\\\n\\\\n        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=workers) as pool:\\\\n            # Submitted in order and popped in order, so the queue both bounds how\\\\n            # many conversions run ahead and preserves the session sequence.\\\\n            pending = deque()\\\\n            submitted = 0\\\\n            for session in range(nsessions):\\\\n                while submitted < nsessions and len(pending) < workers:\\\\n                    pending.append(pool.submit(self.__getitem__, submitted))\\\\n                    submitted += 1\\\\n                yield session, pending.popleft().result()\\\\n\\\\n    def __getitem__(self, session: int):\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Build one session\\\\'s tensors from its per-trial arrays.\\\\n\\\\n        Args:\\\\n            session: index into the per-session lists.\\\\n\\\\n        Returns:\\\\n            {\\\\'neural\\\\': float32 (rows, nneurons), \\\\'input\\\\': float32 (rows, dinput),\\\\n             \\\\'output\\\\': int64 (rows, doutput), \\\\'nneurons\\\\': int}, where rows is the\\\\n            session\\\\'s total timepoints; or None when the session has no trials,\\\\n            which is what the previous version stored for it.\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n        trials = self._neural[session]\\\\n        if len(trials) == 0:\\\\n            return None\\\\n\\\\n        rows = sum(trial.shape[1] for trial in trials)\\\\n        nneurons = trials[0].shape[0]\\\\n\\\\n        # Preallocate and fill, rather than\\\\n        # np.concatenate([trial.T.astype(np.float32) for trial in trials]): that\\\\n        # holds a converted copy of every trial AND the concatenated result at the\\\\n        # same moment. Assigning into the destination casts in place, so neither\\\\n        # temporary is ever allocated. Per-trial inputs and outputs are broadcast\\\\n        # into their row block instead of being np.tile\\\\'d first -- tile built a\\\\n        # (d, T) array per trial only to transpose and copy it again.\\\\n        neural_concat = np.empty((rows, nneurons), np.float32)\\\\n        input_concat = np.empty((rows, self.dinput), np.float32)\\\\n        output_concat = np.empty((rows, self.doutput), np.int64)\\\\n\\\\n        row = 0\\\\n        for trial in range(len(trials)):\\\\n            T = trials[trial].shape[1]\\\\n            neural_concat[row:row + T] = trials[trial].T\\\\n            input_trial = self._input[session][trial]\\\\n            output_trial = self._output[session][trial]\\\\n            # A 1-D per-trial value broadcasts across the block\\\\'s T rows.\\\\n            input_concat[row:row + T] = (input_trial.T if input_trial.ndim == 2\\\\n                                         else input_trial)\\\\n            output_concat[row:row + T] = (output_trial.T if output_trial.ndim == 2\\\\n                                          else output_trial)\\\\n            row += T\\\\n\\\\n        # from_numpy wraps these buffers rather than copying them.\\\\n        return {\\\\n            \\\\'neural\\\\': torch.from_numpy(neural_concat),\\\\n            \\\\'input\\\\': torch.from_numpy(input_concat),\\\\n            \\\\'output\\\\': torch.from_numpy(output_concat),\\\\n            \\\\'nneurons\\\\': nneurons,\\\\n        }\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef train_decoder(neural: list, input: list, output: list, metadata: dict = {}, **kwargs):\\\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n    Trains a common decoder for all sessions to predict output from neural and input data.\\\\n    Jointly learns projection matrices for each session and separate shared decoders for each output dimension.\\\\n\\\\n    Inputs:\\\\n        neural: neural activity. neural is a list of length nsessions, neural[session] is a list\\\\n                of length ntrials[session], and neural[session][trial] is a numpy array of shape (nneurons[session], T[session][trial]),\\\\n                dtype = float32, giving the neural activity for that trial\\\\n        input: variables beyond neural activity the decoder should input, e.g. the stimulis, condition. input is a list of\\\\n                length nsessions, input[session] is a list of length ntrials[session], and input[session][trial] is a numpy array of\\\\n                shape (dinput, T[session][trial]), dtype = float32. with the input variable(s) for each timepoint.\\\\n                dinput is the number of inputs. For discrete inputs, use a one-hot encoding.\\\\n        output: variables we want to decode. output is a list of length nsessions, output[session] is a list of length ntrials[session],\\\\n                and output[session][trial] is a numpy array of shape (doutput, T[session][trial]) of dtype = int with\\\\n                categorical values for each timepoint. Each output[idx, t] is an integer from 0 to ncategories[idx]-1.\\\\n        metadata: optional dict with additional information about the experiment.\\\\n\\\\n        Algorithm hyperparameters can be passed as keyword arguments:\\\\n        npcs: number of projected dimensions to use (default: 10)\\\\n        num_epochs: number of training epochs (default: 200)\\\\n        lr: learning rate (default: 0.001)\\\\n        l1_weight: L1 regularization weight for projection matrices (default: 1e-4)\\\\n        svd_max_samples: maximum number of timepoints to use for SVD initialization (default: 50000).\\\\n                         If a session has more timepoints than this, a random subset will be used.\\\\n        svd_max_neurons: maximum number of neurons to use for SVD initialization (default: 2000).\\\\n                         If a session has more neurons than this, random Gaussian projection will be used.\\\\n                         The final weight is the product of the random projection and SVD matrices.\\\\n        ncategories: optional list of length doutput giving number of categories for each output dimension.\\\\n                     If not provided, will be inferred from data.\\\\n        balanced_loss: if True, use class-weighted cross-entropy loss to compensate for class imbalance.\\\\n                       Weights are inverse of class frequency. Default: False.\\\\n\\\\n    Returns:\\\\n        model: dict with trained decoder parameters. dict with the keys:\\\\n            \\\\'projection\\\\': list of length nsessions, projection[session] is a torch tensor of shape (npcs, nneurons[session])\\\\n            \\\\'decoder\\\\': list of length doutput, decoder[out_dim] is a torch linear layer for that output dimension\\\\n            \\\\'ncategories\\\\': list of length doutput, ncategories[out_dim] is the number of categories for that output\\\\n            \\\\'device\\\\': torch device used for training\\\\n\\\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    model = {\\\\n        \\\\'projection\\\\': [],\\\\n        \\\\'decoder\\\\': [],\\\\n        \\\\'ncategories\\\\': [],\\\\n        \\\\'device\\\\': None\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    npcs = kwargs.get(\\\\'npcs\\\\', 10)\\\\n    num_epochs = kwargs.get(\\\\'num_epochs\\\\', 200)\\\\n    lr = kwargs.get(\\\\'lr\\\\', 0.001)\\\\n    l1_weight = kwargs.get(\\\\'l1_weight\\\\', 1e-4)\\\\n    device_str = kwargs.get(\\\\'device\\\\', None)\\\\n    balanced_loss = kwargs.get(\\\\'balanced_loss\\\\', False)\\\\n\\\\n    nsessions = len(neural)\\\\n    if device_str is not None:\\\\n        device = torch.device(device_str)\\\\n    else:\\\\n        device = torch.device(\\\\'cuda\\\\' if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\\'cpu\\\\')\\\\n\\\\n    model[\\\\'device\\\\'] = device\\\\n    print(\\\\'Using device:\\\\', device)\\\\n\\\\n    # Prepare training data using helper function\\\\n    session_data, dinput, doutput = _prepare_session_data(neural, input, output, device)\\\\n\\\\n    # Determine number of categories for each output dimension\\\\n    if \\\\'ncategories\\\\' in kwargs:\\\\n        ncategories = kwargs[\\\\'ncategories\\\\']\\\\n    else:\\\\n        # Session is the OUTER loop so each one is fetched once for all output\\\\n        # dimensions. With session inner, session_data[session] would rebuild the\\\\n        # same session\\\\'s tensors doutput times. The result does not depend on the\\\\n        # order.\\\\n        max_cat = [0] * doutput\\\\n        for session in range(nsessions):\\\\n            batch = session_data[session]\\\\n            if batch is None:\\\\n                continue\\\\n            for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n                max_cat[out_dim] = max(max_cat[out_dim],\\\\n                                       int(batch[\\\\'output\\\\'][:, out_dim].max().item()))\\\\n        ncategories = [m + 1 for m in max_cat]\\\\n\\\\n    model[\\\\'ncategories\\\\'] = ncategories\\\\n\\\\n    # Compute class weights (always, for use in chance computation and optionally for balanced loss)\\\\n    class_weights = []\\\\n    class_fractions = []  # Store fractions for chance computation\\\\n    # Session outer, output dimension inner, for the same reason as ncategories\\\\n    # above: one fetch per session instead of doutput of them. Summation order\\\\n    # within a class count is unchanged.\\\\n    class_counts_all = [np.zeros(ncategories[out_dim]) for out_dim in range(doutput)]\\\\n    for session in range(nsessions):\\\\n        batch = session_data[session]\\\\n        if batch is None:\\\\n            continue\\\\n        for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n            outputs = batch[\\\\'output\\\\'][:, out_dim].cpu().numpy()\\\\n            for c in range(ncategories[out_dim]):\\\\n                class_counts_all[out_dim][c] += np.sum(outputs == c)\\\\n\\\\n    for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n        class_counts = class_counts_all[out_dim]\\\\n        # Store class fractions for chance computation\\\\n        total = np.sum(class_counts)\\\\n        fractions = class_counts / total\\\\n        class_fractions.append(fractions)\\\\n        # Compute weights as inverse of frequency (with smoothing to avoid division by zero)\\\\n        weights = total / (ncategories[out_dim] * np.maximum(class_counts, 1))\\\\n        # Normalize weights to sum to 1 (so each output dimension is equally weighted)\\\\n        weights = weights / np.sum(weights)\\\\n        class_weights.append(torch.tensor(weights, dtype=torch.float32, device=device))\\\\n\\\\n    model[\\\\'class_weights\\\\'] = class_weights\\\\n    model[\\\\'class_fractions\\\\'] = class_fractions\\\\n\\\\n    if balanced_loss:\\\\n        print(f\\\"Using balanced loss with class weights:\\\")\\\\n        for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n            print(f\\\"  Output {out_dim}: {class_weights[out_dim].cpu().numpy()}\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    # Initialize projection matrices for each session\\\\n    svd_max_samples = kwargs.get(\\\\'svd_max_samples\\\\', 50000)  # Max timepoints to use for SVD initialization\\\\n    svd_max_neurons = kwargs.get(\\\\'svd_max_neurons\\\\', 2000)   # Max neurons to use for SVD initialization\\\\n    projection_layers = []\\\\n    for session in range(nsessions):\\\\n        # Bound once: session_data converts on every subscript, so the four reads\\\\n        # below would otherwise rebuild this session\\\\'s tensors four times.\\\\n        batch = session_data[session]\\\\n        if batch is None:\\\\n            # A session with no trials has no neuron count to size a layer with. Held as\\\\n            # None so that projection_layers stays indexed by session; every read of it is\\\\n            # guarded by the same emptiness check that skips the session\\\\'s data.\\\\n            projection_layers.append(None)\\\\n            continue\\\\n        nneurons = batch[\\\\'nneurons\\\\']\\\\n        proj = nn.Linear(nneurons, npcs, bias=False).to(device)\\\\n\\\\n        # Initialize with SVD for stability (use V from SVD of neural data)\\\\n        # For neural data with shape (n_timepoints, n_neurons), SVD gives principal components in V\\\\n        # Use random subsets if data is too large\\\\n        n_timepoints = batch[\\\\'neural\\\\'].shape[0]\\\\n\\\\n        # Sample timepoints if needed\\\\n        if n_timepoints > svd_max_samples:\\\\n            time_indices = torch.randperm(n_timepoints)[:svd_max_samples]\\\\n            neural_subset = batch[\\\\'neural\\\\'][time_indices, :]\\\\n            print(f\\\"Session {session}: Using {svd_max_samples} of {n_timepoints} timepoints for SVD initialization\\\")\\\\n        else:\\\\n            neural_subset = batch[\\\\'neural\\\\']\\\\n\\\\n        # Use random projection if too many neurons\\\\n        if nneurons > svd_max_neurons:\\\\n            # Create random Gaussian projection matrix: (n_neurons, svd_max_neurons)\\\\n            P = torch.randn(nneurons, svd_max_neurons)\\\\n            # Project neural data: (n_timepoints, n_neurons) @ (n_neurons, svd_max_neurons) = (n_timepoints, svd_max_neurons)\\\\n            neural_projected = neural_subset @ P\\\\n            print(f\\\"Session {session}: Using random projection to {svd_max_neurons} of {nneurons} neurons for SVD initialization\\\")\\\\n\\\\n            # SVD on projected data\\\\n            _, _, V = torch.svd(neural_projected, some=True)\\\\n            # V has shape (svd_max_neurons, min(n_timepoints_subset, svd_max_neurons))\\\\n\\\\n            # Compose: final weight = (P @ V[:, :npcs]).T = V[:, :npcs].T @ P.T\\\\n            n_components = min(npcs, V.shape[1])\\\\n            proj.weight.data[:n_components, :] = (V[:, :n_components].T @ P.T).to(device).clone()\\\\n        else:\\\\n            # Standard SVD without random projection\\\\n            _, _, V = torch.svd(neural_subset, some=True)\\\\n            # V has shape (n_neurons, min(n_timepoints_subset, n_neurons))\\\\n            n_components = min(npcs, V.shape[1])\\\\n            proj.weight.data[:n_components, :] = V[:, :n_components].T.to(device).clone()\\\\n\\\\n        # Initialize remaining components randomly if needed\\\\n        if n_components < npcs:\\\\n            nn.init.orthogonal_(proj.weight.data[n_components:, :])\\\\n\\\\n        projection_layers.append(proj)\\\\n\\\\n    # Initialize separate decoders for each output dimension\\\\n    decoders = []\\\\n    for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n        decoder = nn.Linear(npcs + dinput, ncategories[out_dim]).to(device)\\\\n        decoders.append(decoder)\\\\n\\\\n    # Collect all parameters\\\\n    all_params = []\\\\n    for proj in projection_layers:\\\\n        if proj is None:          # session with no trials, nothing to train\\\\n            continue\\\\n        all_params.extend(proj.parameters())\\\\n    for decoder in decoders:\\\\n        all_params.extend(decoder.parameters())\\\\n\\\\n    optimizer = optim.Adam(all_params, lr=lr)\\\\n\\\\n    # Loss function with reduction=\\\\'none\\\\' for manual weighting (matches torch_brain)\\\\n    criterion = nn.CrossEntropyLoss(reduction=\\\\'none\\\\')\\\\n\\\\n    # Training loop\\\\n    # Data stays on CPU; each session is moved to GPU one at a time.\\\\n    # backward() is called per session to free the computation graph before\\\\n    # loading the next session. Gradients accumulate across sessions.\\\\n    for epoch in range(num_epochs):\\\\n        epoch_loss = 0.\\\\n        optimizer.zero_grad()\\\\n\\\\n        # iter_sessions rather than session_data[session]: every session is\\\\n        # converted once per epoch, and converting the next few on other threads\\\\n        # overlaps that with this session\\\\'s forward and backward pass.\\\\n        for session, batch in session_data.iter_sessions():\\\\n            if batch is None:\\\\n                continue\\\\n            # Move this session\\\\'s data to device\\\\n            neural_batch = batch[\\\\'neural\\\\'].to(device)\\\\n            input_batch = batch[\\\\'input\\\\'].to(device)\\\\n            output_batch = batch[\\\\'output\\\\'].to(device)\\\\n\\\\n            # Forward pass - shared projection\\\\n            projected = projection_layers[session](neural_batch)\\\\n            combined = torch.cat([projected, input_batch], dim=1)\\\\n\\\\n            # Compute loss for each output dimension\\\\n            session_loss = torch.tensor(0., device=device)\\\\n            for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n                logits = decoders[out_dim](combined)\\\\n                target = output_batch[:, out_dim]\\\\n                loss_per_sample = criterion(logits, target)\\\\n\\\\n                if balanced_loss:\\\\n                    # Manual per-sample weighting (matches torch_brain exactly)\\\\n                    sample_weights = class_weights[out_dim][target]\\\\n                    session_loss += (sample_weights * loss_per_sample).sum() / sample_weights.sum()\\\\n                else:\\\\n                    session_loss += loss_per_sample.mean()\\\\n'}]\"\n497:            \"content\": \"[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\\\\nWall time 0.3 seconds\\\\nOutput:\\\\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 25030)\\\\nTotal output lines: 175\\\\n\\\\nWarning: truncated output (original token count: 4493684)\\\\n... 16926160 bytes omitted ...\\\\n\\\\n# DO NOT EDIT THIS FILE LOCALLY. \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Paper's single-trial maximin dF/F and OASIS processing.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    # Undo neuropil contamination while adding its within-trial mean back, as\\\\n+    # in Only\\\\n+the on-track interval from each trial-start event up to (but not including) its\\\\n+teleport event is retained.\\\\n+\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+\\\\n+from __future__ import \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Use aligned timestamps, which correctly account for two-plane sessions.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    timestamps =                 starts: np.ndarray, stops: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    outcomes = np.zeros(len(starts), dtype=np.int8)\\\\n+    for trial, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(starts, stops)):\\\\n+        left = np.searchsorted(reward_timestamps, timestamps[start], side=\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        right = np.searchsorted(reward_timestamps, timestamps[stop], side=\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        outcomes[trial] = right > left\\\\n+    return outcomes\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _reward_zone_labels(position: np.ndarray, rzone: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                        starts: np.ndarray, stops: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                        experiment_day: int) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Recover the active zone robustly from the aligned rzone event stream.\\\\n+\\\\n+    The aligned rzone channel is an integrated event/count channel, not a zone\\\\n+    label.  Its nonzero samples occur inside the active zone.  The experimental\\\\n+    protocol has one condition for the session, or two conditions split after\\\\n+    trial 30 on switch days.  Taking the modal observed zone in each condition\\\\n+    also fills reward-omission trials where the event channel can be all zero.\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    observed = for trials {left}:{right}\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        labels[left:right] = np.bincount(valid, minlength=3).argmax()\\\\n+    return labels\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _bad_lick_trials(lick: np.ndarray, starts: \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Paper's single-trial maximin dF/F and OASIS processing.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    # Undo neuropil contamination while adding its within-trial mean back, as\\\\n+    # in _distance_class(position: np.ndarray, zone: int) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    start, end = ZONE_STARTS[zone], ZONE_ENDS[zone]\\\\n+    distance = np.where(position < start, position - start,\\\\n+ Discretization bins:   - 0:  < -50 cm   - 1: -50 to -10 cm   - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm   - 3: 0 cm   - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm   - 5: +10 to +50 cm   - 6: > +50 cm - Absolute position in corridor, time-varying. Discretized into 5 equal-sized bins spanning the 450 cm track:   - 0: < 90 cm   - 1: 90 to 180 cm   - 2: 180 to 270 cm   - 3: 270 to 360 cm   - 4: > 360 cm - Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:   - 0: < 2 cm/s   - 1: 2-10 cm/s   - 2: 10-20 cm/s   - 3: 20-40 cm/s   - 4: > 40 cm/s - Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes - Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C - Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes  ## Target Data Format  - Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`. - All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:  ```python data = {     \\\\'\\\"\\\\'\\\"\\\\'neural\\\\'\\\"\\\\'\\\"\\\\': [  # They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.   - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:     - visual: contrast, position, orientation     - audio: frequency, amplitude     - behavior: running speed, pupil size   - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial.    - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder   - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series.   - **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity   - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal\\\\'s output behavior or properties of the stimulus.   - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it.    - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position   - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying   - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series.   - **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice). - **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.   - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.  - **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from. - **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.  - **input_names**: Names of each input variable.   - Order should match order of `input` dimensions.   - **output_names**: Names of each output variable.   - Order should match order of `output` dimensions. - **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.   - Order should match categorical `output` values.  - **metadata**: Descriptive information.    - `task_description`: Concise description of the task   - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event   - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after.    - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial   - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`  ## Decoder Reference  - Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate. - Optional arguments:   - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).   - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.   - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).  ### Purpose  These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure: 1. text(r.output);\\n\"}\n====\nSTEP 28\nexec\nARGS: {\"input\":\"const patch = \\\"*** Begin Patch\\\\n*** Add File: /app/convert_data.py\\\\n+#!/usr/bin/env python3\\\\n+\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Convert the Sosa et al. (2025) NWB release for neural decoding.\\\\n+\\\\n+The conversion follows the paper's calcium-processing methods: manually curated\\\\n+Suite2p ROIs are neuropil corrected, converted to trial-wise maximin dF/F,\\\\n+smoothed, deconvolved with OASIS, and screened for putative interneurons.  Only\\\\n+the on-track interval from each trial-start event up to (but not including) its\\\\n+teleport event is retained.\\\\n+\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+\\\\n+from __future__ import annotations\\\\n+\\\\n+import argparse\\\\n+import os\\\\n+import pickle\\\\n+import re\\\\n+from pathlib import Path\\\\n+\\\\n+import h5py\\\\n+import numpy as np\\\\n+from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter1d, maximum_filter1d, minimum_filter1d\\\\n+from suite2p.extraction import dcnv\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+BEHAVIOR = \\\\\\\"processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries\\\\\\\"\\\\n+OPHYS = \\\\\\\"processing/ophys\\\\\\\"\\\\n+ZONE_STARTS = np.array([80.0, 200.0, 320.0], dtype=np.float32)\\\\n+ZONE_ENDS = ZONE_STARTS + 50.0\\\\n+ZONE_CENTERS = (ZONE_STARTS + ZONE_ENDS) / 2.0\\\\n+SWITCH_DAYS = {3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14}\\\\n+NOMINAL_RATE_HZ = 15.5078125\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _decode_scalar(value) -> str:\\\\n+    if isinstance(value, bytes):\\\\n+        return value.decode(\\\\\\\"utf-8\\\\\\\")\\\\n+    return str(value)\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _natural_key(path: Path):\\\\n+    subject = re.search(r\\\\\\\"sub-m(\\\\\\\\d+)\\\\\\\", str(path))\\\\n+    session = re.search(r\\\\\\\"ses-(\\\\\\\\d+)\\\\\\\", path.name)\\\\n+    return (int(subject.group(1)), int(session.group(1)))\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _read_behavior(group, name: str) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    return np.asarray(group[f\\\\\\\"{name}/data\\\\\\\"])\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _trial_bounds(behavior) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\\\n+    starts = np.flatnonzero(_read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"trial_start\\\\\\\") > 0)\\\\n+    stops = np.flatnonzero(_read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"teleport\\\\\\\") > 0)\\\\n+    if len(starts) != len(stops):\\\\n+        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\"Unmatched trial starts ({len(starts)}) and teleports ({len(stops)})\\\\\\\")\\\\n+    if np.any(stops <= starts):\\\\n+        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"A teleport did not follow its corresponding trial start\\\\\\\")\\\\n+    return starts, stops\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _sampling_rate(behavior) -> float:\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Use aligned timestamps, which correctly account for two-plane sessions.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    timestamps = np.asarray(behavior[\\\\\\\"position/timestamps\\\\\\\"])\\\\n+    rate = float(1.0 / np.median(np.diff(timestamps)))\\\\n+    if not np.isclose(rate, NOMINAL_RATE_HZ, rtol=0, atol=1e-4):\\\\n+        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\"Unexpected aligned sampling rate: {rate} Hz\\\\\\\")\\\\n+    return rate\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _trial_outcomes(reward_timestamps: np.ndarray, timestamps: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                    starts: np.ndarray, stops: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    outcomes = np.zeros(len(starts), dtype=np.int8)\\\\n+    for trial, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(starts, stops)):\\\\n+        left = np.searchsorted(reward_timestamps, timestamps[start], side=\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        right = np.searchsorted(reward_timestamps, timestamps[stop], side=\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        outcomes[trial] = right > left\\\\n+    return outcomes\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _reward_zone_labels(position: np.ndarray, rzone: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                        starts: np.ndarray, stops: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                        experiment_day: int) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Recover the active zone robustly from the aligned rzone event stream.\\\\n+\\\\n+    The aligned rzone channel is an integrated event/count channel, not a zone\\\\n+    label.  Its nonzero samples occur inside the active zone.  The experimental\\\\n+    protocol has one condition for the session, or two conditions split after\\\\n+    trial 30 on switch days.  Taking the modal observed zone in each condition\\\\n+    also fills reward-omission trials where the event channel can be all zero.\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    observed = np.full(len(starts), -1, dtype=np.int8)\\\\n+    for trial, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(starts, stops)):\\\\n+        mask = rzone[start:stop] > 0\\\\n+        if np.any(mask):\\\\n+            event_position = float(np.median(position[start:stop][mask]))\\\\n+            observed[trial] = int(np.argmin(np.abs(ZONE_CENTERS - event_position)))\\\\n+\\\\n+    boundaries = [0, min(30, len(starts)), len(starts)] if experiment_day in SWITCH_DAYS else [0, len(starts)]\\\\n+    labels = np.empty(len(starts), dtype=np.int8)\\\\n+    for left, right in zip(boundaries[:-1], boundaries[1:]):\\\\n+        valid = observed[left:right]\\\\n+        valid = valid[valid >= 0]\\\\n+        if len(valid) == 0:\\\\n+            raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\"Cannot infer reward zone for trials {left}:{right}\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        labels[left:right] = np.bincount(valid, minlength=3).argmax()\\\\n+    return labels\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _bad_lick_trials(lick: np.ndarray, starts: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                     stops: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Paper criterion: >30% of frame samples have cumulative lick count >2.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    return np.asarray([\\\\n+        np.mean(lick[start:stop] > 2) > 0.30\\\\n+        for start, stop in zip(starts, stops)\\\\n+    ], dtype=bool)\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _dff_and_events(fluorescence: np.ndarray, neuropil: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                    rate_hz: float) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Paper's single-trial maximin dF/F and OASIS processing.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    # Undo neuropil contamination while adding its within-trial mean back, as\\\\n+    # in reward_relative.preprocessing.dff (neu_coef=0.7).\\\\n+    corrected = fluorescence - 0.7 * neuropil\\\\n+    corrected += 0.7 * neuropil.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\\\\n+\\\\n+    baseline = gaussian_filter1d(corrected, 15, axis=1)\\\\n+    baseline = minimum_filter1d(baseline, 300, axis=1)\\\\n+    baseline = maximum_filter1d(baseline, 300, axis=1)\\\\n+    dff = (corrected - baseline) / np.abs(baseline)\\\\n+    dff = gaussian_filter1d(dff, 2, axis=1).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\\\\n+    events = dcnv.oasis(dff, 2000, 0.7, rate_hz)\\\\n+    return dff, events\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _distance_class(position: np.ndarray, zone: int) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    start, end = ZONE_STARTS[zone], ZONE_ENDS[zone]\\\\n+    distance = np.where(position < start, position - start,\\\\n+                        np.where(position > end, position - end, 0.0))\\\\n+    return np.select(\\\\n+        [distance < -50, distance < -10, distance < 0, distance == 0,\\\\n+         distance <= 10, distance <= 50],\\\\n+        [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5], default=6,\\\\n+    ).astype(np.int8)\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _output_matrix(position: np.ndarray, speed: np.ndarray, lick: np.ndarray,\\\\n+                   zone: int, rewarded: int) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    absolute_position = np.digitize(position, [90, 180, 270, 360]).astype(np.int8)\\\\n+    speed_class = np.digitize(speed, [2, 10, 20, 40]).astype(np.int8)\\\\n+    lick_binary = (lick > 0).astype(np.int8)\\\\n+    timepoints = len(position)\\\\n+    return np.vstack([\\\\n+        _distance_class(position, zone),\\\\n+        absolute_position,\\\\n+        speed_class,\\\\n+        lick_binary,\\\\n+        np.full(timepoints, zone, dtype=np.int8),\\\\n+        np.full(timepoints, rewarded, dtype=np.int8),\\\\n+    ])\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def convert_session(path: Path) -> tuple[dict, dict]:\\\\n+    with h5py.File(path, \\\\\\\"r\\\\\\\") as nwb:\\\\n+        behavior = nwb[BEHAVIOR]\\\\n+        starts, stops = _trial_bounds(behavior)\\\\n+        rate_hz = _sampling_rate(behavior)\\\\n+        timestamps = np.asarray(behavior[\\\\\\\"position/timestamps\\\\\\\"])\\\\n+        position = _read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"position\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        speed = _read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"speed\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        lick = _read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"lick\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        environment = _read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"environment\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        trial_number = _read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"trial number\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        rzone = _read_behavior(behavior, \\\\\\\"reward_zone\\\\\\\")\\\\n+\\\\n+        subject = _decode_scalar(nwb[\\\\\\\"general/subject/subject_id\\\\\\\"][()])\\\\n+        experiment_day = int(_decode_scalar(nwb[\\\\\\\"general/session_id\\\\\\\"][()]))\\\\n+        n_planes = len(np.unique(nwb[f\\\\\\\"{OPHYS}/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/planeIdx\\\\\\\"][:]))\\\\n+        is_cell = nwb[f\\\\\\\"{OPHYS}/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell\\\\\\\"][:, 0].astype(bool)\\\\n+        roi_indices = np.flatnonzero(is_cell)\\\\n+        if len(roi_indices) == 0:\\\\n+            raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"Session has no manually curated cells\\\\\\\")\\\\n+\\\\n+        outcomes = _trial_outcomes(\\\\n+            np.asarray(behavior[\\\\\\\"Reward/timestamps\\\\\\\"]), timestamps, starts, stops\\\\n+        )\\\\n+        zones = _reward_zone_labels(position, rzone, starts, stops, experiment_day)\\\\n+        bad_lick = _bad_lick_trials(lick, starts, stops)\\\\n+\\\\n+        f_dataset = nwb[f\\\\\\\"{OPHYS}/Fluorescence/plane0/data\\\\\\\"]\\\\n+        fneu_dataset = nwb[f\\\\\\\"{OPHYS}/Neuropil/plane0/data\\\\\\\"]\\\\n+\\\\n+        events_by_trial: list[np.ndarray] = []\\\\n+        sum_x = np.zeros(len(roi_indices), dtype=np.float64)\\\\n+        sum_x2 = np.zeros(len(roi_indices), dtype=np.float64)\\\\n+        sum_xy = np.zeros(len(roi_indices), dtype=np.float64)\\\\n+        sum_y = 0.0\\\\n+        sum_y2 = 0.0\\\\n+        sample_count = 0\\\\n+\\\\n+        # Process all trials for the paper's session-wide speed-correlation\\\\n+        # interneuron screen, including trials whose lick sensor was faulty.\\\\n+        for start, stop in zip(starts, stops):\\\\n+            fluorescence = np.asarray(f_dataset[start:stop, :], dtype=np.float32)[:, roi_indices].T\\\\n+            neuropil = np.asarray(fneu_dataset[start:stop, :], dtype=np.float32)[:, roi_indices].T\\\\n+            dff, events = _dff_and_events(fluorescence, neuropil, rate_hz)\\\\n+            if not np.all(np.isfinite(dff)) or not np.all(np.isfinite(events)):\\\\n+                raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"Non-finite neural activity after calcium processing\\\\\\\")\\\\n+            events_by_trial.append(events)\\\\n+\\\\n+            trial_speed = speed[start:stop].astype(np.float64, copy=False)\\\\n+            dff64 = dff.astype(np.float64, copy=False)\\\\n+            sum_x += dff64.sum(axis=1)\\\\n+            sum_x2 += np.square(dff64).sum(axis=1)\\\\n+            sum_xy += dff64 @ trial_speed\\\\n+            sum_y += trial_speed.sum()\\\\n+            sum_y2 += np.square(trial_speed).sum()\\\\n+            sample_count += len(trial_speed)\\\\n+\\\\n+    numerator = sample_count * sum_xy - sum_x * sum_y\\\\n+    denominator = np.sqrt(\\\\n+        (sample_count * sum_x2 - np.square(sum_x))\\\\n+        * (sample_count * sum_y2 - sum_y * sum_y)\\\\n+    )\\\\n+    speed_correlation = np.divide(\\\\n+        numerator, denominator, out=np.zeros_like(numerator), where=denominator > 0\\\\n+    )\\\\n+    putative_interneuron = speed_correlation > 0.5\\\\n+    keep_neuron = ~putative_interneuron\\\\n+    if not np.any(keep_neuron):\\\\n+        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"Interneuron screen removed every cell\\\\\\\")\\\\n+\\\\n+    neural_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\\\\n+    input_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\\\\n+    output_trials: list[np.ndarray] = []\\\\n+    retained_source_trials: list[int] = []\\\\n+    for trial, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(starts, stops)):\\\\n+        if bad_lick[trial]:\\\\n+            continue\\\\n+        timepoints = stop - start\\\\n+        trial_env = int(np.rint(np.median(environment[start:stop])))\\\\n+        source_trial_number = float(np.median(trial_number[start:stop]))\\\\n+        previous_outcome = int(outcomes[trial - 1]) if trial > 0 else 0\\\\n+        input_trial = np.vstack([\\\\n+            np.arange(timepoints, dtype=np.float32) / np.float32(rate_hz),\\\\n+            np.full(timepoints, trial_env, dtype=np.float32),\\\\n+            np.full(timepoints, source_trial_number, dtype=np.float32),\\\\n+            np.full(timepoints, previous_outcome, dtype=np.float32),\\\\n+        ])\\\\n+        output_trial = _output_matrix(\\\\n+            position[start:stop], speed[start:stop], lick[start:stop],\\\\n+            int(zones[trial]), int(outcomes[trial]),\\\\n+        )\\\\n+        neural_trials.append(np.ascontiguousarray(events_by_trial[trial][keep_neuron]))\\\\n+        input_trials.append(input_trial)\\\\n+        output_trials.append(output_trial)\\\\n+        retained_source_trials.append(trial)\\\\n+\\\\n+    session_info = {\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"source_file\\\\\\\": str(path.resolve()),\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"subject\\\\\\\": subject,\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"experiment_day\\\\\\\": experiment_day,\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"sampling_rate_hz\\\\\\\": rate_hz,\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"n_imaging_planes\\\\\\\": n_planes,\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"source_trial_count\\\\\\\": len(starts),\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"retained_trial_count\\\\\\\": len(neural_trials),\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"excluded_faulty_lick_trials\\\\\\\": np.flatnonzero(bad_lick).astype(int).tolist(),\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"retained_source_trials\\\\\\\": retained_source_trials,\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"manually_curated_roi_count\\\\\\\": int(len(roi_indices)),\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"excluded_putative_interneuron_count\\\\\\\": int(putative_interneuron.sum()),\\\\n+        \\\\\\\"retained_neuron_count\\\\\\\": Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. These are defensive checks found during data exploration. The neural/behavior mismatch occurs in a small number of sessions.\n\n## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are:\n1. **Loading NWB files** (`pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` and reading large arrays) — I/O bound\n2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n3. **Full conversion** — loads every NWB file again and extracts all trial data\n4. **Saving the .pkl file**\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The NWB files are large (contain full neural recordings). Conversion is otherwise quite fast\n\n## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n\nii. N/A\n\niii. switching to encourage stable assignments\n    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n    n_states = len(zone_names)\n    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n    pstay = .9\n    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n\n    # transition log-probabilities\n    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n\n    results = {}\n\n    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n\n        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n        # viterbi forward pass\n        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n            for k in range(n_states):\n                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n\n        # backtrack\n        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n\n        # convert state indices to zone names\n        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n\n        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n\n        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n\n    return results\n The paper’s key time-domain neural signal is trial-wise ΔF/F, smoothed at two imaging samples; spatial analyses instead use deconvolved activity and exclude speed below 2 cm/s.
